# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-08-26_TotalExtraction_87352988.pdf
NOTICE OF PROPOSED TRADE OF REAL PROPERTY 
 
COMES NOW the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and pursuant to the provisions of Wyoming 
Statute Section 15-1-112(b)(ii), provides public notice of a proposed trade of real property 
owned by the City of Cheyenne, described Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1st 
Filing, and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 2nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, 
consisting of 419.37 acres and having an appraised value of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC.  The 
City-owned parcel is proposed to be traded for real property owned by Mariah, LLC, described 
as All of Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the Union Pacific Railroad right-of-way, Township 
12 North, Range 70 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, consisting of 543.76 acres 
and having an appraised value of $1,060,000.00.  A public hearing on the proposed trade of real 
property will be held by the Governing Body in the City Council Chambers at 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, WY 82001, on Monday, August 26, 2019 at 6:00 P.M.  The public is invited 
to offer comments on the proposed trade of real property either in person at the meeting or by 
mailing comments to the City Clerk, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY 82001. 
 
 
Publish: 
August 9, 16 and 23, 2019. 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-08-26_TotalExtraction_19985697.pdf
NOTICE OF PROPOSED SALE OF REAL PROPERTY 
 
COMES NOW the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and pursuant to the provisions of Wyoming 
Statute Section 15-1-112(b)(i)(D), provides public notice of a proposed sale of undeveloped 
public property described below with an appraised value of $293,000.00 to John J. Koehler, 
without calling for bids on the property for the reasons that such sale will provide development 
of a private enterprise within a Community/Regional Center as identified by the 2014 update to 
the Cheyenne Comprehensive Plan, and will serve as a catalyst for economic development within 
a Community/Regional Center.  The property is legally described as:  A 1.46 acre portion of Lot 
1, Block 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1st Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming.  The proposed price will be $293,000.00.  A public hearing on this proposed sale will 
be held by the Governing Body in City Council Chambers at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, 
WY 82001, on Monday, August 26, 2019 at 6:00 P.M.  The public is invited to offer comments 
on the proposed sale of real property either in person at the meeting or by mailing comments to 
the City Clerk, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY 82001. 
 
 
Publish: 
August 9, 16 and 23, 2019. 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-08-26_TotalExtraction_71095383.pdf
Approved s to
ORDINANCE NO.
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 8.64, SMOKING IN PUBLIC
PLACES,
WITHIN
TITLE
8,
HEALTH
AND
SAFETY,
OF
THE
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY Of THE CITY Of CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That one section of Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, of Title 8, Health and
Safety, is hereby amended as set out in this Ordinance.
Sections currently existing in Chapter
8.64 not set out within this ordinance remain unchanged.
8.64.020
- Definitions.
As used in this chapter, the following terms shall have the following meanings, unless the
context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended:
“Business” means any legal entity including, but not limited to, sole proprietorship,
partnership, joint venture, corporation or other business entity whether formed for profit-making
or nonprofit purposes, including retail establishments where goods or services are sold, as well as
professional
corporations
and
other
entities
where
legal,
medical,
dental,
engineering,
architectural or other professional services are delivered.
“City-owned youth athletic facilities” includes, but is not limited to, facilities where youth
soccer, baseball, skateboarding and any other youth activities occur, whether the facility is
enclosed or not.
“Electronic smoking device” means any device that can be used to deliver aerosolized
or vaporized nicotine, tobacco, plant, or other product, including hookah and marijuana,
whether natural or synthetic to the person inhaling from the device, including, but not
limited to. an e-cigarette, e-cigar, e-pipe, vane pen or e-hookah excluding any device
designed to deliver prescription medication.
“Employee” means any person who is employed by an employer in consideration for direct
or indirect monetary wages or profit, and a person who volunteers his or her services for a
nonprofit entity.
“Employer” means a person, business, partnership, association, corporation, trust, nonprofit
entity, or a municipal corporation that employs the services of one or more individual persons.
“Enclosed area” means all space between a floor and ceiling that is enclosed on all sides by
solid walls or windows, exclusive of doorways, which extend from the floor to the ceiling.
“Fire department” means the city of Cheyenne fire and rescue department.

“Health care facility” means an office or institution providing care or treatment of diseases,
whether physical, mental, or emotional, or other medical, physiological, or psychological
conditions including, but not limited to, hospitals, rehabilitation hospitals or other clinics,
including weight control clinics, nursing homes, homes for the aging or chronically ill,
laboratories, and offices of surgeons, chiropractors, physical therapists, physicians, dentists, and
all specialists with these professions. This definition shall include all waiting rooms, hallways,
private rooms, semi-private rooms, and wards with health care facilities.
“Outdoor patio” means an outdoor area, open to the air at all times, that is either:
1.
Enclosed by a roof or other overhead covering and not more than two walls or other
side coverings; or
2.
Has no roof or other overhead covering at all regardless of the number of walls or other
side coverings.
“Performance hall” means any area or facility that is primarily used for the exhibition of any
motion picture, stage, drama, lecture, musical recital or other similar performances including, but
not limited to, concert halls and theaters.
“Place of employment” means an area under the control of an employer which is intended
for occupancy by employees during the course of employment including, but not limited to, work
areas, employee lounges, restrooms, conference rooms, meeting rooms, classrooms, employee
cafeterias, waiting areas, offices, stairways, and hallways. A private residence is not a “place of
employment” unless it is used as a licensed child care, licensed adult day care, health care or pre
school facility, or any other aforementioned business.
“Private club” means those organizations or entities defined as a “club” in Wyo. Stat.
Section 12-1-101 (a)(iii), which are not open to the public.
“Public place” means an enclosed area to which the public is invited or in which the public
is permitted including, but not limited to, banks, and other financial institutions, publicly funded
buildings,
public conveyances,
recreational
facilities,
taverns,
lounges,
bars,
educational
facilities, health care facilities, laundromats, public transportation facilities, reception areas,
restaurants, retail food production and marketing establishments, including grocery stores,
supermarkets and stores where food items are sold for on-premises or off-premises consumption,
retail service establishments, retail stores, shopping malls, sports arenas, theaters, and waiting
rooms. A private residence is not a “public place” unless it is used as a licensed child care,
licensed adult day care, health care or pre-school facility.
“Public transportation” means, but is not limited to, any enclosed mode of public
transportation, including a train, passenger bus, school bus or other vehicle used to transport
pupils, and taxi; or a vehicle that is owned by, or operated by a business and is open to the
public, including tour vehicles, passenger buses or vans regularly used to transport customers.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, a private vehicle not open to the public or not used for the
transportation of the public during the times of use, including a private passenger vehicle, a
private charter or rental of a limousine, bus or van, shall not be considered public transportation.

“Restaurant” means an eating establishment including, but not limited to, coffee shops,
cafeterias, and public and private school cafeterias, which gives or offers for sale food to the
public, guests, or employees, as well as kitchens and catering facilities in which food is prepared
on the premises and served elsewhere. The term “restaurant” shall include any tavern, lounge or
bar area within the restaurant.
“Retail sales” means establishments engaged in the sale of goods or merchandise to the
general public for personal or household consumption and rendering services incidental to the
sale of such goods. A retail sales establishment is usually a place of business and is engaged in
activity to attract the general public to make purchases.
“Shopping mall” means an enclosed public walkway or hail area that serves to connect retail
sale or professional service establishments.
“Smoke” or “smoking” means inhaling, exhaling, burning, or carrying any lighted or
heated cigar, cigarette, pipe, or any other lighted or heated tobacco, nicotine, or other
product intended for inhalation, including hookah and marijuana, whether natural or
synthetic the inhaling, exhaling, burning or carrying of any lighted tobacco product, weed, plant
or other combustible product in a smoking instrument or the placement of smoking instruments
containing a lighted tobacco, nicotine, plant or other product, weed, plant or other combustible
product in an ashtray or other receptacle and allowing smoke to diffuse into the air. This
definition does not include incense.
“Smoke” or “smoking” also includes the use of an
electronic smoking device.
“Smoking instrument” means any cigar, cigarette, electronic smoking device, pipe, hookah
or water pipe, or any other smoking device.
“Sports arena” means sports pavilions, stadiums, gymnasiums, health spas, swimming poois,
roller and ice rinks, bowling alleys, and other similar places where members of the general
public assemble to engage in physical exercise, participate in athletic competition, or witness
sports or other events.
“Tavern, lounge or bar” means any establishment open to the public whose primary source
of revenue is from the sale of alcoholic liquor and malt beverages for consumption by guests on
the premises and in which the serving of food is only incidental to the consumption of such
beverages including, but not limited to, taverns, nightclubs, cocktail lounges, and cabarets.
“Youth” means any person who has not attained eighteen (18) years of age.
Section 2.
This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.
FIRST READING:
SECOND READING:_______
THIRD AND FINAL READING:________________________________
Marian J. Orr, Mayor

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows:
(a)
That Sunnyside Southern Baptist Church, owner of the south one hundred fifty
feet (150’) of Tract 119, all of Tract 120, and those portions of Tracts 135 and 136
north ofthe right of way for U.S. Hwy. No 30, and all of vacated Cheyenne Street
(60’), and the west ‘/2 (25’) of vacated Fillmore Ave., adjacent to said Tracts,
Sunnyside Addition fourth filing, situate in a portion of the SE 1/4 SE1/4 of
Section 27, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th PM, Laramie County,
Wyoming, have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex
the real property described herein and to make it a part thereot and
(b)
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as
Exhibit A, said map prepared by John J. Studley, a Professional Land Surveyor,
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 4828; and
(c)
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and
(d)
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and
(e)
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason,
be furnished; and
(1)
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MU
Mixed Use by the Laramie County Commissioners; and
(g)
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the
entire annexed area.
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy ofthis annexation
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Enginee?s Office to add said area to
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Section 4.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section ofthis ordinance is declared
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the

remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.
Section 5.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

REQUIRED DOCUMENTS SUBMISSION: The following required documents have been submitted and
reviewed by staff.
•
Master Application; Petition to Annex (Signed by at least 51% of property owners who
own at least 51% of land to be annexed); Annexation Map; Soils Report (requirement
fulfilled by deferral request); Signed clearance from the Board of Public Utilities
•
On August 5, 2019, the Agent for the Applicant submitted a letter stating the
Applicant wishes to withdraw the annexation petition and the accompanying
zone change and subdivision applications. This letter is attached to this report.
RECOMMENDATION: Although the Applicant wishes to withdraw the annexation petition due to their
inability to provide the City Fire Department’s demand for a fire safety inspection report of the existing
gymnasium located within the annexation site, should the Governing Body wish to move forward with the
annexation petition, Staff recommends approval of the annexation application.
According to Wyoming State Statute 15-1-408, annexations are effective upon the publication of the
ordinance (not the recording of the annexation map); however, most City and County systems only
update their maps and services upon the recording of the annexation map with the County Clerk.
Therefore, instead of conditioning approval of the annexation, Staff recommends that the applicants
provide a recordable annexation map to the City prior to the approval of the annexation Ordinance on
reading
— so that the City may record a corrected and suitable annexation map as close to the publication
of the Ordinance as possible. lithe recordable annexation map is not provided to Staff prior to 3rd
reading, Staff recommends the Governing Body postpone approval until the corrected map is provided.
Through the review process, Staff has identified the following necessary correction to the map:
1.
The two Legal Descriptions provided on the map are inconsistent with one another.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:
This annexation application is for one (1) parcel of land at approximately 4 acres located south of the
intersection of Fillmore Avenue and Charles Street. This application was submitted along with a zone
change application and preliminary plat application. The land to be annexed would need to be given a
City zone to allow any development actions. The zone change application proposes changing the County
MU Mixed-use zone to NUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis.
PlanCheyenne is supportive of annexing this land into the City. This land is located within the Urban
Service Boundary and is designated Public and Quasi-Public on the future land use map. The Future Land
Use Map anticipates the area to develop with uses related to community services such as educational
facilities, civic buildings and places of worship. The future land uses anticipated in the Public and Quasi-
Public land use category are best served by being in City limits and connected to City services.
As with any annexation expansion there would be an impact on service provision within the City.
However, as this is a small expansion adjacent to current City services, the impact on City services would
be minor. The property is contiguous to current City limits to the North, East, and West (see Map 4 on
page 7).
The Fire Department has expressed significant concern regarding the safety of the existing gymnasium
located on the subject property. The Fire Department is requiring a code summary evaluation from a
Registered Fire Protection Engineer of the gymnasium prior to being brought into the City. The evaluation
would address occupancy class, required fire systems, appliances, fire access as well as confirming the
ability of the road to support the heaviest piece of fire apparatus, or 75,000 pounds. The report would
include a proposed timeline for which any fire-code deficiencies or violations would be required to be
brought into compliance. The Fire Department would not support Annexation until the code summary
evaluation is reviewed by their Department and found complete.
However, due to meeting the criteria found in Wyoming Statute 15-1-402, staffrecommends
approvalofthis annexation.

ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)):
COMMON WELFARE — An annexation ofthe area is for the protection ofthe health, safety, and welfare of
the persons residing in the area and/n the Uty or town.
The subject property is currently connected to City water and sewer without the benefit of an
approved Outside Users Agreement with the City’s Board of Public Utilities (BOPU). Annexation
would allow the Applicant to purchase City water and sewer at a lower rate. City police and fire
services would be available immediately, which serves the public safety and welfare. Therefore,
the annexation of this property would increase the health, safety, and welfare of the residents of
the Cheyenne area. However, the Fire Department has expressed significant concern regarding
the safety of the existing gymnasium located on the subject property. The Fire Department is
requiring a code summary evaluation from a Registered Fire Protection Engineer of the
gymnasium prior to being brought into the City. The evaluation would address occupancy class,
required fire systems, appliances, fire access as well as confirming the ability of the road to
support the heaviest piece of fire apparatus, or 75,000 pounds. The report would include a
proposed timeline for which any fire-code deficiencies or violations would be required to be
brought into compliance.

PART OF THE Cm’ — The urban development ofthe area sought to be annexed would be constituted a

This property will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the City. The
property is adjacent to both developed and vacant land within City boundaries. Charles Street
immediately to the north and Cleveland Avenue to the west are also in City limits and under City
maintenance. The properties location along U.S. Highway 30 makes this parcel a natural
geographic part of the City. The employees of the church as well as the church members are
likely to live in the Cheyenne area and are a part of the social and economic fabric that is
Cheyenne.
SERVICE EXTENSION LOGICAL EXPANSION
— The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible
addition to the annexing Uty or town and the extension ofbasic and other services customarily available
to residents ofthe city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed.
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City
services normally available to businesses and residents will also be available. Police and fire
protection would be extended upon annexation. The subject property is currently connected to
City water and sewer.

public utilities within the territory a summary ofthe proposed annexation report as required under

ANNEXATION REPORT DATA: In compliance with W.S. 5 15-1-402(c) the following information is
provided:
(i)
A map is not required for this report based on the absence of exercising the authority
granted under W.S. 15-3-202 (b) (ii).

The total estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the landowners by the
City related to the annexation is approximately $0. Additional on- and off-site improvements
may be required with subsequent development actions. On-site improvements for fire safety
will be required with annexation.
(iii)
Services customarily available to City residents include water, sewer, sanitation, police and
fire protection. Police and Fire services would be provided immediately upon the recording of
the annexation. Water and sewer services are provided upon application and payment to the
Board of Public Utilities, with sanitation services occurring concurrently. Provision of these
services is initiated by the property owner. Future development would require extension of
water and sewer services, which are available to the subject property.

Fees for water, sewer, and sanitation services are a variable amount for commercial
properties, based upon volume and meter size.

The current property tax mill levy imposed is 66.52 and projected property tax mill levy
imposed by the city is 71.52.

The infrastructure described in (ii) above includes improvements required within the existing
boundaries of the municipality

From:
Jim Schamerhom
Sent:
Tuesday, June 4, 2019 10:24 AM
To:
Jack Studley
Cc:
Bob Mason
Subject:
RE: Sunnyside Baptist Church
Good morning Jack,
Thank you for the research and follow up on information from the county regarding their evaluation of the church
gymnasium.
After reading 2010 and 2012 letters, our feeling Is the county misinterpreted the Intent of the code and specific sections
regarding the need for fire sprinkler requirements in the basement of the gymnasium.
Section 9.117 of the 1982 Uniform Fire Code (Occupancy Classification) identifies the gymnasium as an A-3 Occupancy.
“Any building or portion of a building having an assembly room with an occupant load of less than 300 without a stage,
including buildings used for educational purposes and not classed as a Group E or Group B, Division 2 Occupancy”. UFC,
Section 10.309 C C) item 2, identifies the requirement for fire sprinklers in basements of assemblies over 1500 square
feet.
As promised, I’ve discussed annexation with the Fire Marshal and have come to the following conclusion; As originally
requested we will need a code summary evaluation from a Registered Fire Protection Engineer of those buildings being
brought into the city. The analysis shall address occupancy class, required fire systems, appliances, fire access as well as
confirming the ability of the toad to support our heaviest piece of apparatus or 75,000 lbs. In the report, the FPE fin
consultation with the church) may include a proposed time line for correction of identified non-code issues.
We hope this provides a clear understanding of our concerns and expectations for resolving the annexation of the
church property.
Please contact our office with any questions,
Best regards,
Jim Schamerhorn
Fire Plans Examiner/Fire Inspector
Cheyenne Fire & Rescue
307-637-6312
jschamerhorn@cheyennecity.org
From: Jack Studley
Sent: Wednesday, May 29, 2019 3:40 PM
To: Bob Mason <bmason@cheyennecity.org>; Jim Schamerhorn <jschamerhorn@cheyennecity.org>
Cc: Tawn Hillenbrand <thillenbrand@cheyennecity.org>
Subject: Sunnyside Baptist Church
Bob & Jim
Attached are a couple of pieces of correspondence with the Laramie County Planning and Development Office
concerning the Sunnyside Baptist Church gymnasium and Its use.
Hopefully we can research this a little further and get together to see if we can’t create a resolution to the issues.
1

John I. Studley, PLS
615 West Dale Blvd.
Cheyenne, WY 82009
(307) 287-4755
Jklz03l8@gmai1.com
5 August 2019
TO:
Cheyenne Planning Office
Attn: Susanna Montana
3966 Archer Parkway
Cheyenne, WY 82001
Re:
Sunnyside Baptist Church.
Dear Ms. Montana,
Sunnyside Baptist Church have submitted applications/petitions to your office to annex and re
zone a portion, and replat all of their lands in eastern Cheyenne for the purpose of subdividing the lands in
an appropriate manner for disposal of the vacant lands.
The acting Fire Marshall, Bob Mason has informed me that they will not waive the requirement
for a Fire Protection Certification on the existing gymnasium.
Sunnyside Baptist Church has decided that these requirements are unnecessary for the following
reasons:
•
Documentation from Laramie County has afready been provided to show the gymnasium
was constructed in 1981 in compliance with existing building code standards.
•
There is no proposed change to the construction or function of the existing church
complex.
•
The required Fire Protection Certification is cost prohibitive ($5,000).
•
The gymnasium would be subject to closure if not brought up to your current building code
standards ($50,000+). Again, cost prohibitive.
Therefore, they have decided to remain in the County, and hereby withdraw the referenced
applications/petitions for Annexation, Zone Change, Preliminary Plat and Final Plat.
Agent for Sunnyside Baptist Church
